# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-11

Full expanded open-source creative-AI discovery across 98 observed fields, 836 repository queries and 24 model tasks, supplemented by live primary-source research outside GitHub. This edition provides 44 detailed guides and 47 additional eligible screened discoveries. Six guides complete earlier screened entries, and OpenFilm has a concrete 0.3.2 release update; other first guides are baselines, not claims that those tools launched today. Coverage includes local audio/video, code-assisted films, neural/agent 3D and XR, typography, CAD/fabrication, pose-driven performance, game AI and a GitLab-hosted MLX video editor. Software permissions are separated from model, asset, host and paid-service terms; restricted or unproven workflows remain excluded or pending. Every included entry has a dated six-check assessment. Documentation and published examples were reviewed; no discovered app was installed or run, and no new entry is presented as independently recommended. The existing 2,400-entry weekly review was already complete this week; new entries are checked now and final whole-library coverage is verified after export. Field/source-group findings and failed or size-bounded source captures are disclosed; no exhaustive census is claimed. Verified collection: 836/836 repository queries attempted; 836 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 96 bounded/truncated. All 24 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 43 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Images & design | Invoke’s current primary documentation describes its open-source generation canvas. Prisme and other new image workflows are separately screened; a model catalog or polished UI is not a quality verdict. [Source 1](https://invoke.ai/) |

OpenFilm 0.3.2 has Windows/export changes; Phosphene advanced to 5.0.18 during review, with an optional Pro-only speed-up assessed separately from the free open-code route. [Source 1](https://openreelio.com/) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) [Source 4](https://github.com/mrbizarro/Phosphene/releases/tag/v5.0.18) |

New speech/stem tools keep software grants separate from voice rights, trained weights, optional cloud services and listening-quality evidence. | Browser tools & web media | The WebLLM primary paper supplies browser-inference context. Three-Live, browser OCR, style transfer and stem separation require their own backend/device and model-term checks. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15803) |

## Search and review counts

98 creative fields; 836/836 repository queries attempted; 24/24 model-task queries attempted. 45233 distinct source candidates and 1022 model leads. 44 detailed profiles and 47 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 96 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Current raw GitLab documentation and complete Apache-2.0 grant support a new JoyAI-Video-Edit MLX guide. Its quantized-memory descriptions conflict and weight terms remain separate. Openedit was also read; Xotion’s full PolyForm Noncommercial grant remains excluded. This is a bounded investigation, not all of GitLab. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **codeberg** · searched: Normal public HTTP reads returned current Imaginer and Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion project pages. The search service reported robots restrictions for this host; those restrictions and the limited two-project investigation are disclosed. No new complete software-grant or hardware-quality clearance is claimed from these pages. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Fresh public diffuser Cargo.toml and complete MIT text were read. The active dependency still names an author-local diffusers-rs path, a concrete reproducibility gap. Search-service host restrictions limit broad discovery; this read is not an exhaustive host search or a newly validated installation. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Live npm litert-ocr metadata, PyPI northcel/game-localizer/gameloc records and the NuGet TensorStack.Media version index were inspected. NorthCel’s registry and source guide distinguish published and future spec versions; a versioned manimvox record is not claimed as the latest package. Registry metadata is a discovery signal, not a complete license or quality review. A later live NorthCel recheck confirmed PyPI 0.7.1/Python >=3.10, matching the refreshed guide; the release changes documentation rather than rendering semantics. [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/northcel/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/game-localizer/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/gameloc/json) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Fresh Krita AI primary documentation and reviewed Blender, ComfyUI and MCP integration sources identify actual creative model/agent operations. BlenderNeRF prepares neural-training data; model-backed ComfyUI analysis and neural transcript integrations are different roles. Host, model and addon licenses remain separate. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://github.com/MaximeRaafat/BlenderNeRF) [Source](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/)
- **project-sites** · searched: Current Phosphene, Kaestral, Kerfline, SAT and Three-Live primary sites were checked, alongside primary context for every observed field. Some sites were shells or failed, and four PDF captures were bounded; these gaps are explicit. Phosphene was 5.0.17 at the initial capture, then released 5.0.18 during collection. The new Pro-only speed-up and separate pack access are disclosed rather than attributed to the free route. [Source](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html) [Source](https://github.com/mrbizarro/Phosphene/releases/tag/v5.0.18)
- **research-code** · searched: BrickGPT/BrickWright author pages, released source and complete code grants were reviewed. ReversibleHalftoning has a custom commercial-notice/citation grant; LayerPano3D includes a non-commercial rasterizer; OpenLRM includes restricted NVIDIA code. These are watchlist/excluded workflows, not eligible entries inferred from their paper or root badge. [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source](https://github.com/MenghanXia/ReversibleHalftoning/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/LayerPano3D/blob/master/submodules/diff-gaussian-rasterization/LICENSE.md) [Source](https://github.com/3DTopia/OpenLRM/blob/main/LICENSE_NVIDIA)
- **international** · searched: Current Chinese and multilingual AICON/StoryGen/subtitle sources, Spanish MTTN instructions, Arabic localization documentation, TMD notation and Russian media-agent sources broaden language coverage. Local inference, optional hosted translation, font/rendering constraints and regional model restrictions remain specific evidence questions; language or research origin does not determine tier. The researched polymath-gen is archived and is excluded from new entries. [Source](https://github.com/869413421/ai-moive-studio) [Source](https://github.com/0xsline/StoryGen-Atelier) [Source](https://github.com/la-matatena/mttn-autosubs-tool) [Source](https://abdallah28r.github.io/taarib/en/) [Source](https://tmdlang.github.io/Tmd-TS/) [Source](https://github.com/rtynbirf/polymath-gen)

## Kerfline

3D printing & generative CAD · Vector graphics, illustration & textures · AI printmaking, risograph and editioned art

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Preparing stained-glass, silhouette and laser-cut concept patterns from a clear illustration, with a visible redraw/review loop.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README and project site show source, AI redraw, vector and cut examples. They are developer demonstrations; neither those examples nor a connected SVG certify your material, kerf or minimum feature size. [Source](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the official source, use Python 3.10+ and install the editable package. Install Potrace separately; librsvg is optional for previews. Configure OpenAI and Anthropic access locally; an existing supported Claude CLI plan can replace the review API. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The software grant is Apache-2.0. GPT-image and Claude are separately operated, proprietary model services; no local open-weight generation route is documented. Paid OpenAI image calls and Anthropic review calls, or a supported existing Claude CLI subscription for review; local tracing adds compute. Author estimates are not a price guarantee. [Source](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. No vector-stage review loop, kerf compensation or physical fabrication validation is implemented in the documented workflow. Provider prices and author cost estimates may change.

### How it uses AI

A fabrication-specific pipeline asks an image model to redraw an illustration, uses Claude vision to score fidelity and connected outlines, bridges isolated regions and traces the chosen raster into SVG. [Source](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/)

### Introduction

A fabrication-specific pipeline asks an image model to redraw an illustration, uses Claude vision to score fidelity and connected outlines, bridges isolated regions and traces the chosen raster into SVG. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

### What it is good for

Preparing stained-glass, silhouette and laser-cut concept patterns from a clear illustration, with a visible redraw/review loop. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

### Demo & examples

The README and project site show source, AI redraw, vector and cut examples. They are developer demonstrations; neither those examples nor a connected SVG certify your material, kerf or minimum feature size. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

### Install

Clone the official source, use Python 3.10+ and install the editable package. Install Potrace separately; librsvg is optional for previews. Configure OpenAI and Anthropic access locally; an existing supported Claude CLI plan can replace the review API. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the official source, use Python 3.10+ and install the editable package. Install Potrace separately; librsvg is optional for previews. Configure OpenAI and Anthropic access locally; an existing supported Claude CLI plan can replace the review API.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline.git
```


```sh
cd kerfline
```


```sh
pip install -e .
```


```sh
kerfline my-image.png --output-dir output/
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

1. Choose an illustration with clear boundaries and configure provider access in your local environment.
2. Run a small redraw; inspect the selected redraw, bridges and the summary verdict rather than trusting the AI score alone.
3. Compare both VTracer and Potrace SVGs; check minimum features, scale and kerf in your fabrication software before cutting.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, Potrace CLI, project Python dependencies; optional librsvg. The documented package-manager examples cover macOS and Linux.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux dependency examples are documented; a Windows installation path is not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://www.kerfline.build/)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** The software grant is Apache-2.0. GPT-image and Claude are separately operated, proprietary model services; no local open-weight generation route is documented.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The software grant is Apache-2.0. GPT-image and Claude are separately operated, proprietary model services; no local open-weight generation route is documented.
- **Cost:** Paid OpenAI image calls and Anthropic review calls, or a supported existing Claude CLI subscription for review; local tracing adds compute. Author estimates are not a price guarantee.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

### Limitations

No vector-stage review loop, kerf compensation or physical fabrication validation is implemented in the documented workflow. Provider prices and author cost estimates may change. [Source 1](https://github.com/rainesdrew/kerfline/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kerfline.build/)

## Kaestral

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · Motion capture & character animation · Typography, fonts & layout

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Turning a talking-head recording into an editable short, refining word captions and motion graphics, then exporting a video or an editing interchange file.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official README contains editor and prompt-to-timeline GIFs. These demonstrate the author workflow; claimed test totals and marketing do not establish independent quality. [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the Windows release installer for the bundled editor and engine. The npm/MCP route instead needs Node/npm and FFmpeg on PATH. Connect your chosen supported coding agent; review which media/context it sends to its model provider. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** GPL-3.0 editor code derives from Palmier Pro; NOTICE retains upstream attribution. Whisper/FFmpeg/Remotion and the chosen AI agent have their own licenses and access conditions. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Pro/Max generation, dubbing and upscaling are explicitly waitlisted, not shipped features. Local rendering/transcription does not make an external LLM private or free. Third-party notice terms need checking for redistributed builds.

### How it uses AI

A Windows timeline editor exposes edit operations to an LLM through MCP and performs local Whisper transcription, frame inspection and media analysis for AI-assisted cuts and captions. [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

### Introduction

A Windows timeline editor exposes edit operations to an LLM through MCP and performs local Whisper transcription, frame inspection and media analysis for AI-assisted cuts and captions. [Source 1](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

### What it is good for

Turning a talking-head recording into an editable short, refining word captions and motion graphics, then exporting a video or an editing interchange file. [Source 1](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

### Demo & examples

The official README contains editor and prompt-to-timeline GIFs. These demonstrate the author workflow; claimed test totals and marketing do not establish independent quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

### Install

Use the Windows release installer for the bundled editor and engine. The npm/MCP route instead needs Node/npm and FFmpeg on PATH. Connect your chosen supported coding agent; review which media/context it sends to its model provider. [Source 1](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the Windows release installer for the bundled editor and engine. The npm/MCP route instead needs Node/npm and FFmpeg on PATH. Connect your chosen supported coding agent; review which media/context it sends to its model provider.

```sh
claude mcp add kaestral -- npx kaestral
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/prabindersinghh/Kaestral-pro/blob/main/docs/FEATURES.md)

1. Install the release and import a short recording you can share with the chosen agent.
2. Ask for a concise edit and word captions; inspect the timeline, transcript and frame decisions and correct them manually.
3. Export a small H.264 test or interchange project and check timing, fonts, sound and destination-editor compatibility.

## Media Toolkit

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

The README says bundled notices include FFmpeg GPL terms; the referenced notice filename was not available at the root source URL during this review. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Downloads/site support can change independently. Automatic transcripts need proofreading. The missing source notice path is an unresolved distribution-documentation gap, not proof that the MIT root grant is invalid.

### How it uses AI

A desktop/local-web media workspace combines downloading with genuine local Whisper speech recognition and exports transcripts, timestamped notes and subtitles. Caption retrieval alone is a conventional fallback, not an AI result. [Source](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/releases/latest)

### Install

Choose the release for Windows, Apple Silicon macOS or Linux x86-64. Bundles include Python, FFmpeg and yt-dlp; Whisper weights download when needed. Source installs need Python 3.11+ and the documented requirements. Release builds are currently unsigned. [Source 1](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/releases/latest)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Choose the release for Windows, Apple Silicon macOS or Linux x86-64. Bundles include Python, FFmpeg and yt-dlp; Whisper weights download when needed. Source installs need Python 3.11+ and the documented requirements. Release builds are currently unsigned.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit.git
```


```sh
cd media-toolkit
```


```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python tools/fetch_ffmpeg.py
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python run.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/releases/latest)

1. Open Transcript and drop a short local audio/video file, so the Whisper route is actually exercised.
2. Choose language/model, inspect timestamps and correct names, omissions and translated words against the audio.
3. Export text, SRT, VTT or JSON and import it into your editor; use remote media only with appropriate access and rights.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/AnotherAH/media-toolkit/releases/latest)

- **Hardware:** Speech downloads are documented as 75 MB to 3 GB; optional NVIDIA cuBLAS download is 528 MB. Minimum RAM/VRAM and total installed disk are not documented.
- **Software:** Source: Python 3.11+ (3.13 recommended). Releases carry the runtime. Linux folder picker uses zenity/kdialog; AppImage may require FUSE. Optional Windows NVIDIA support downloads separately.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11; macOS 12+ Apple Silicon; Linux x86-64. macOS/Linux transcription uses CPU in this documented app, not inferred Metal support.

## BrickGPT

AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design · 3D, reconstruction & assets · 3D printing & generative CAD

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Exploring brick sculpture and small architectural concepts from text, while retaining a brick-by-brick representation for inspection.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official paper site and README show generated structures, colored variants and human assemblies. Those results apply to the authors’ evaluated builds; they do not certify an arbitrary new sculpture. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the official repository, uv and access to the gated Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct base. Run uv sync. The infer/render route additionally requires the ImportLDraw submodule, the separately downloaded background image and LDraw parts. Physics-based checking needs Gurobi and its license; the documented connectivity fallback is less accurate. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The authors state MIT for their code, model and StableText2Brick dataset. The gated Meta base retains its own Llama terms; ImportLDraw, FlashTex, LDraw data and Gurobi retain separate grants. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Connectivity is not the same as physics-based stability. Do not interpret MIT project code as a blanket license for the base model, third-party assets or Gurobi. RAM, VRAM and disk minima are not documented.

### How it uses AI

A language model fine-tuned for text-to-brick generation proposes brick placements, with stability checking and rollback options; the repository also supplies separate rendering, texturing and mesh-to-brick modules. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### Introduction

A language model fine-tuned for text-to-brick generation proposes brick placements, with stability checking and rollback options; the repository also supplies separate rendering, texturing and mesh-to-brick modules. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

### What it is good for

Exploring brick sculpture and small architectural concepts from text, while retaining a brick-by-brick representation for inspection. The official paper site and README show generated structures, colored variants and human assemblies. Those results apply to the authors’ evaluated builds; they do not certify an arbitrary new sculpture. ### Install

Obtain the official repository, uv and access to the gated Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct base. Run uv sync. The infer/render route additionally requires the ImportLDraw submodule, the separately downloaded background image and LDraw parts. Physics-based checking needs Gurobi and its license; the documented connectivity fallback is less accurate. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the official repository, uv and access to the gated Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct base. Run uv sync. The infer/render route additionally requires the ImportLDraw submodule, the separately downloaded background image and LDraw parts. Physics-based checking needs Gurobi and its license; the documented connectivity fallback is less accurate.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT.git
```


```sh
cd BrickGPT
```


```sh
uv sync
```


```sh
uv run infer --help
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/BrickGPT/blob/main/pyproject.toml)

1. Resolve model access and choose physics-based Gurobi checking or explicitly accept the weaker connectivity fallback.
2. Follow the CLI inference example with a small object prompt; inspect the resulting brick coordinates and preview.
3. Check part availability and assembly, then validate a small real build before scaling the design. Treat texture and mesh conversion as separate workflows with their own setup.

## BrickWright / BrickLayer

AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design · 3D, reconstruction & assets · 3D printing & generative CAD

The repository was created on 2026-10-16. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Iterative text- or image-guided brick concept design with a visible generate, render, validate and revise loop.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author project page includes orbitable designs and downloadable LDraw examples. Its reported validity rates are scoped author benchmark results; they are not physical strength or assembly certification for your prompt. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install the BrickWright CLI with uv, or obtain the source. Connect an external coding agent or configure an API provider locally. Download the LDraw parts library and set LDRAW_LIBRARY_PATH if you need previews; rendering is disabled without it. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT project code. Bundled part data derive from LDraw, BrickNet and Rebrickable and retain their notices. Agent/API subscriptions and proprietary models are separate. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The validity tests cover specific geometric/connectivity conditions. Real load bearing, inventory availability and unrestricted redistribution of every part dataset are not established by those tests.

### How it uses AI

A coding-agent framework builds brick sculptures through a Python SDK, validates real-part placement, checks collisions and floating/disconnected elements, and exports editable LDraw models. [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)

### Introduction

A coding-agent framework builds brick sculptures through a Python SDK, validates real-part placement, checks collisions and floating/disconnected elements, and exports editable LDraw models. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)

### What it is good for

Iterative text- or image-guided brick concept design with a visible generate, render, validate and revise loop. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)

### Install

Install the BrickWright CLI with uv, or obtain the source. Connect an external coding agent or configure an API provider locally. Download the LDraw parts library and set LDRAW_LIBRARY_PATH if you need previews; rendering is disabled without it. [Source 1](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) [Source 3](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/brickwright/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/AvaLovelace1/brickwright/blob/main/packages/bricklayer/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the BrickWright CLI with uv, or obtain the source. Connect an external coding agent or configure an API provider locally. Download the LDraw parts library and set LDRAW_LIBRARY_PATH if you need previews; rendering is disabled without it.

```sh
uv tool install brickwright
```


```sh
brickwright generate "a detailed cruise ship"
```


```sh
brickwright generate --help
```

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Organic shapes, curves and non-stud connection techniques are outside the shipped connection model. Software geometry reports do not prove real clutch strength or a safe physical structure.

### How it uses AI

An MCP server gives an LLM semantic brick placement, catalog, preview, validation and LDraw export tools, instead of making it manipulate a CAD interface through clicks. [Source](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An MCP server gives an LLM semantic brick placement, catalog, preview, validation and LDraw export tools, instead of making it manipulate a CAD interface through clicks. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Agent-guided stud-connected architectural brick concepts, editable MPD files, part lists and step-by-step previews. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a generated fortress, a viewer session and imported model renders. The author explicitly says the design task remains hard and the connection model is stud-only. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the official Python tool with uv, download the LDraw library with lego-mcp install-library, and register the executable in your MCP client. Claude Desktop is the documented inline-preview route; the standalone agent needs separate Anthropic access. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the official Python tool with uv, download the LDraw library with lego-mcp install-library, and register the executable in your MCP client. Claude Desktop is the documented inline-preview route; the standalone agent needs separate Anthropic access.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP.git
```


```sh
cd BrickBuilderMCP
```


```sh
uv tool install --from . lego-mcp
```


```sh
lego-mcp install-library
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

1. Connect the server in your MCP client and start a very small stud-aligned model.
2. Prefer the documented valid-placement and high-level wall/room tools; inspect connectivity, overlap and structural reports after revisions.
3. Save a project, export MPD/LDraw and inspect the part list before trying a physical assembly.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author documents roughly 150 MB for the LDraw library. Minimum RAM, VRAM and full installed storage are not documented.
- **Software:** uv Python toolchain; an MCP-capable LLM client, or Anthropic API for the standalone agent; downloadable LDraw parts.
- **Platforms:** macOS is author-tested, Linux is documented. Windows via WSL is suggested but explicitly untested.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT server code; LDraw data and your LLM client/provider are separate dependencies.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT server code; LDraw data and your LLM client/provider are separate dependencies.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Organic shapes, curves and non-stud connection techniques are outside the shipped connection model. Software geometry reports do not prove real clutch strength or a safe physical structure. [Source 1](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/jonx/BrickBuilderMCP/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

An agent skill and MCP server use vision to identify photographed parts, keep an inventory and help an LLM design constrained brick models with rendered build instructions. [Source](https://github.com/elcodabra/lego-build-inventory/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An agent skill and MCP server use vision to identify photographed parts, keep an inventory and help an LLM design constrained brick models with rendered build instructions. [Source 1](https://github.com/elcodabra/lego-build-inventory/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Making a small concept from parts you already own and checking the proposed inventory before producing PNG or vertical/horizontal MP4 instructions. [Source 1](https://github.com/elcodabra/lego-build-inventory/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the official supported agent plugin/MCP setup. Local use needs Node 20+; the first server launch installs dependencies and Chromium. FFmpeg is needed only for MP4. The hosted alternative requires its OAuth sign-in and stores data with its own services. [Source 1](https://github.com/elcodabra/lego-build-inventory/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official supported agent plugin/MCP setup. Local use needs Node 20+; the first server launch installs dependencies and Chromium. FFmpeg is needed only for MP4. The hosted alternative requires its OAuth sign-in and stores data with its own services.
3. The final command is the documented Claude Code route. Choose scripts/install.sh desktop for a printed local MCP configuration instead; use only one route.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/elcodabra/lego-build-inventory.git
```

```sh
cd lego-build-inventory
```

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

The backends expose different capabilities: the browser does not run Python ASR/plugin workers, so a successful server example does not establish identical browser features. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Editing/alignment and ASR have additional companions and worker limits. Browser and native sampling need not produce identical bytes. Independent creator-use and cross-device quality checks remain incomplete.

### How it uses AI

A multilingual neural text-to-speech and voice-cloning runtime offers native CUDA, MLX, C++/ggml and in-browser WebGPU backends with a shared voice-studio API. [Source](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

### Introduction

A multilingual neural text-to-speech and voice-cloning runtime offers native CUDA, MLX, C++/ggml and in-browser WebGPU backends with a shared voice-studio API. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

### What it is good for

Creating a consented voice-over, auditioning lines in several languages and exporting speech for a film, interactive character or installation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The author supplies a browser-local playground, model card and evaluation pages. The backends expose different capabilities: the browser does not run Python ASR/plugin workers, so a successful server example does not establish identical browser features. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

### Install

For a no-server audition use the official WebGPU playground in a compatible browser. For Apple Silicon, obtain the recursive source, run uv sync for zonos3-mlx, fetch the GGUF companions and use the MLX launcher. Linux CUDA and native ggml have separate build and model instructions; do not mix old companions with a current backbone. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For a no-server audition use the official WebGPU playground in a compatible browser. For Apple Silicon, obtain the recursive source, run uv sync for zonos3-mlx, fetch the GGUF companions and use the MLX launcher. Linux CUDA and native ggml have separate build and model instructions; do not mix old companions with a current backbone.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini.git
```


```sh
cd ZONOS3-Mini
```


```sh
uv sync --locked --package zonos3-mlx
```


```sh
hf download Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini --include "gguf/*" --local-dir servers/ggml
```


```sh
servers/mlx/scripts/serve.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

1. Choose a backend whose documented capabilities cover the intended speech task.
2. Open the local studio at 127.0.0.1:1919, select a default voice or your consented reference and generate a short passage.
3. Listen for pronunciation, language drift, clipping and voice consistency; export WAV and review the result in your editor before longer narration.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini) [Source 3](https://www.zyphra.com/our-work/zonos3-mini) [Source 4](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/mlx/README.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/ggml/README.md) [Source 6](https://github.com/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/blob/main/servers/web/README.md)

- **Software:** Python 3.12 and uv for Python servers; Apple MLX on Apple Silicon; Linux NVIDIA/CUDA for the SGL server; CMake/Rust/submodules for ggml; WebGPU for browser use. Read the chosen backend guide.
- **Platforms:** Linux NVIDIA, Apple Silicon MLX, native CPU/Metal/CUDA/Vulkan ggml and WebGPU browser routes are documented separately. This is not proof of every OS/device/backend combination.

### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/mrbizarro/Phosphene/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) [Source 3](https://phosphene.video/docs/) [Source 4](https://github.com/mrbizarro/Phosphene/blob/main/docs/MANUAL_INSTALL.md) [Source 5](https://github.com/mrbizarro/Phosphene/releases/tag/v5.0.18)

- **Hardware:** The author documents smaller LTX video from 16 GB unified memory, higher modes at larger memory tiers, and about 27.5 GB for LTX Q4 weights. Setup needs 18 GB free including a 15 GB reserve; optional H3 is about 75 GB and Music Studio about 11 GB. These are author figures, not universal limits. The optional 5.0.18 Pro speed-up is enabled at 32 GB+ and downloads a separate 1.2 GB add-on; these figures do not replace the base workflow requirements.
- **Software:** Apple Silicon/MLX. Native app and Pinokio installation are documented; the manual contains the source route. A universal minimum macOS version was not established in this review.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon M1–M5 Macs. Intel Macs and Windows are explicitly unsupported. A separate Apple-Silicon Linux port is best-effort, not a general Linux support guarantee.

## OpenFilm — 0.3.2 update

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · Motion capture & character animation · AI agents for code-authored media production

Version 0.3.2 (November 9) improves Windows project switching/network-share media, canvas/asset coordination and effects/subtitle retention in editing-project exports. This updates the October guide; it is not a launch-today claim.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making a code-driven product film or explainer, then handing off editable sources or an interchange project to another editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author README links a launch film and examples. Release 0.3.2 documents Windows project switching/network-share fixes and exports that retain effects and visible captions; those concrete changes justify revisiting the October guide. [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/releases/tag/v0.3.2) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/examples) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** CLI use requires Node 22.2+, FFmpeg and Git for saved Studio versions; the first run downloads Chromium. Official desktop apps for macOS/Windows bundle the runtime. Source development instead documents Node 22.18+, pnpm 9, Git and FFmpeg. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/releases/tag/v0.3.2) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/examples) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT format/CLI/Studio/desktop code; third-party notices, media-library assets, agent models and generated-media providers retain their own terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/releases/tag/v0.3.2) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/examples) [Source](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Deterministic-frame checks detect repeatability and loading faults; they do not establish visual quality. Interchange exports need checking in your destination editor, and hosted generation incurs separate costs.

### How it uses AI

A coding-agent video system represents editable tracks as HTML and timed pages, renders them with Chromium and provides a local timeline/canvas editor for both agent and manual changes. Author tests are not independent proof of musical quality, audio-device latency or every mobile browser.

### How it uses AI

A browser music studio exposes editable notes, sounds and device settings to a coding agent through MCP, so a player and agent can take turns making and revising a song. [Source](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

### Introduction

A browser music studio exposes editable notes, sounds and device settings to a coding agent through MCP, so a player and agent can take turns making and revising a song. [Source 1](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source 3](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

### What it is good for

Co-composing a small loop, changing an instrument or effect in a visible session, and keeping human and agent takes distinguishable. [Source 1](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source 3](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

### Demo & examples

The official site and README describe a live studio and example sessions. Authored clips and compatibility checks are documentation evidence, not an independent listening or latency test. [Source 1](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source 3](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

### Install

Clone the official repository and start its Node server; no package installation or build is documented for the base studio. For agent editing, use your local copy and register server/mcp.js with the chosen supported MCP client. [Source 1](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source 3](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the official repository and start its Node server; no package installation or build is documented for the base studio. For agent editing, use your local copy and register server/mcp.js with the chosen supported MCP client.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub.git
```


```sh
cd overdub
```


```sh
node server/serve.js
```


```sh
claude mcp add overdub -- node /path/to/overdub/server/mcp.js
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/overdubstudio/overdub/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://overdubstudio.com/) [Source 3](https://overdubstudio.com/site/docs/)

1. Open http://localhost:3279/app/ and make or load a short take.
2. Connect the local MCP bridge, ask the agent for a small audible change, and inspect the notes and settings it edits.
3. Audition the result, undo unwanted changes and render/export only after checking any sample-kit permissions.

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

2. Use the official documented distribution or build the source with its Git submodules. Building requires CMake 3.15+, Ninja, a C++20 compiler and Vulkan SDK 1.4; the rendering GPU needs Vulkan 1.3. Follow the README’s compiler/dependency instructions for the selected OS.
3. Provision the README-pinned FFmpeg 8.1 and VST3 SDK 3.8.1 paths before configuring CMake. Add whisper.cpp 1.8.7 only for the subtitle route. Then use the documented Ninja configure/build commands and run the resulting editor. On Windows use a Visual Studio developer prompt.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/Zovelik/QuickEdit.git
```


```sh
cd QuickEdit
```


```sh
cmake -G Ninja -S . -B out/build/x64-Release -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release
```


```sh
cmake --build out/build/x64-Release
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Zovelik/QuickEdit/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Zovelik/QuickEdit/blob/master/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md)

1. Open a small voice-over project and verify import/playback.
2. Connect the MCP-capable agent, request one explicit cut/title operation and inspect the timeline.
3. Review auto subtitles manually, export a short test and verify sound, shader effects and codec playback.

A Rust/WASM browser application runs a six-stem BS-RoFormer model through WebGPU and provides editable playback, solo/mute, loops and stem export. ### Introduction

A Rust/WASM browser application runs a six-stem BS-RoFormer model through WebGPU and provides editable playback, solo/mute, loops and stem export. Use the author’s published browser page with a compatible WebGPU implementation, or follow the Rust/Trunk source-build instructions. ### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. - **Hardware:** The documented model download is about 350 MB. Minimum system RAM/VRAM and complete browser storage needs are not documented.
- **Software:** WebGPU-capable browser for use. Source builds use Rust, wasm32-unknown-unknown and pinned Trunk 0.21.14, plus the documented build tools.
- **Platforms:** The author tested Chrome on macOS with specific Intel/AMD GPUs. Other named WebGPU browsers/devices are suggested but explicitly not all tested.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. An MCP interface is not a built-in generative model. Rig behavior, external AI edits, scripting and each exported engine integration require workflow-specific checking.

### How it uses AI

A 2D skeletal-animation editor exposes its editing operations over MCP so an external AI agent can rig or animate the same document a human edits; runtimes carry the result into games or interactive pages. [Source](https://github.com/UXCentury/open-bone/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://openbone.io/) [Source](https://openbone.io/getting-started/quick-start/)

The MCP package is a separate documented agent entry point; choose only the runtime adapter matching your destination engine. [Source 1](https://github.com/UXCentury/open-bone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://openbone.io/) [Source 3](https://openbone.io/getting-started/quick-start/)

```sh
git clone https://github.com/UXCentury/open-bone.git
```


```sh
cd open-bone
```


```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm dev
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/UXCentury/open-bone/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://openbone.io/) [Source 3](https://openbone.io/getting-started/quick-start/)

3. Review motion and state behavior, save a new .obone and test its target-runtime export before a complete character.

### First project

### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/project/northcel/) [Source 4](https://pypi.org/pypi/northcel/json)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python >=3.10 according to the current PyPI 0.7.1 record. Native execution is Linux x86-64/spec 0.3; newer documents use the labelled Python reference or optional Vello. Source-only/perception extras have their own dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Native Linux x86-64 requires glibc 2.39+ in the current documentation. macOS, Windows and other Linux architectures use the Python reference; they do not inherit native GPU performance.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE) [Source 2](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) [Source 4](https://pypi.org/project/northcel/)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** MIT or Apache-2.0 code; language/compiler/runtime dependencies and an external AI authoring model retain separate terms. No image-model weights are bundled.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. MIT or Apache-2.0 code; language/compiler/runtime dependencies and an external AI authoring model retain separate terms. No image-model weights are bundled.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/project/northcel/)

### Limitations

The documentation mixes release and unreleased spec examples. A reference-renderer result is not native-runtime performance evidence, and reproducible bytes do not prove that the drawing matches the brief. The new 0.7.1 release explicitly changes documentation only: no rendering semantics changed, the native runtime stays 0.4.0 and northcel-vello stays 0.7.0. Do not treat its version bump as a new rendering capability. [Source 1](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/project/northcel/) [Source 4](https://github.com/syver777/northcel/releases/tag/v0.7.1)

Some optional Seed-VC setups require compatibility patches. A model list is not a license or stability approval. The newer 0.2.4 release adds project organization and character swapping in clips, but the latter requires trama_trocar_personagem_video.json plus the named H3 character-swap LoRA in ComfyUI. That optional model/workflow and its terms are not cleared by the MIT application grant.

### How it uses AI

A canvas-based agent plans and runs concrete local ComfyUI workflows for image, video and audio tasks, while a separately configured language-model endpoint supplies the planning/chat intelligence. 3. The source server opens http://127.0.0.1:5190. In Settings, configure the selected provider and verify the running ComfyUI address. The Windows start-trama.bat is an alternative, not an additional requirement.


```sh

```


```sh
npm start
```

Local browser compute/model downloads; no hosted generation API is required. [Source](https://github.com/reiinakano/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://reiinakano.github.io/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs) [Source](https://magenta.tensorflow.org/blog/2018/12/20/style-transfer-js/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The documented speed/size trade-off reduces detail in the lighter models. Current dependency compatibility and independent reproduction remain unverified; an old research date alone is not an Experimental verdict.

### How it uses AI

Two TensorFlow.js neural networks encode an arbitrary reference style and apply it to a content image directly in a browser, including weighted combinations of styles. [Source](https://github.com/reiinakano/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://reiinakano.github.io/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs) [Source](https://magenta.tensorflow.org/blog/2018/12/20/style-transfer-js/)

### Introduction

Two TensorFlow.js neural networks encode an arbitrary reference style and apply it to a content image directly in a browser, including weighted combinations of styles. [Source 1](https://github.com/reiinakano/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://reiinakano.github.io/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs) [Source 3](https://magenta.tensorflow.org/blog/2018/12/20/style-transfer-js/)

### What it is good for

Auditioning painterly treatments and style blends on a photograph without uploading it to a generation API. [Source 1](https://github.com/reiinakano/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://reiinakano.github.io/arbitrary-image-stylization-tfjs) [Source 3](https://magenta.tensorflow.org/blog/2018/12/20/style-transfer-js/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the official source. Install Node 18+, npm and FFmpeg, configure a local backend environment for the chosen provider, and follow the documented combined launcher or backend/frontend manual startup. Vertex video requires an enabled Google Cloud project/API, not just the software clone.
3. Configure the backend provider environment first. The root launcher checks Node/npm, installs frontend/backend dependencies on first use, and starts both servers. Read the README manual route when using a different shell.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/0xsline/StoryGen-Atelier.git
```


```sh
cd StoryGen-Atelier
```


```sh
chmod +x start_servers.sh
```


```sh
./start_servers.sh
```

A self-hosted video knowledge base indexes speech, frames and multimodal embeddings so a creator can search a media collection or individual scenes with natural language. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Finding a spoken passage or a described scene in a private reference/media archive before assembling an edit. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes an author setup video and describes background indexing. The app remains explicitly not production-ready; successful indexing and fluent search do not guarantee semantic retrieval accuracy. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Docker Desktop and the official setup guide/installer, or configure the source Compose setup. Set the allowed media folder and choose local Ollama, hosted Gemini or no AI. Generate local session/encryption secrets and map the media folder before docker compose up. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Docker Desktop and the official setup guide/installer, or configure the source Compose setup. Set the allowed media folder and choose local Ollama, hosted Gemini or no AI. Generate local session/encryption secrets and map the media folder before docker compose up.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind.git
```


```sh
cd edit-mind
```


```sh
docker compose up
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

1. Complete the README configuration before starting Compose; choose which local media folder containers may read.
2. Open localhost:3745 (127.0.0.1 for the documented Safari route), create an account, add the mapped folder and rescan a small sample.
3. Try several known-answer searches, compare returned scenes against the originals and inspect transcription/indexing errors before adding a large archive.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Docker Desktop/Compose; the container stack includes web/background/ML services and ChromaDB. Optional Ollama model or Gemini access is configured separately.
- **Platforms:** Docker Desktop and macOS/Linux terminal or Windows Git Bash/WSL setup routes are documented. GPU/model backend compatibility remains route-specific.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/LICENSE.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** AGPL-3.0
- **Weights:** AGPL-3.0 application code; Docker Desktop, selected models, Gemini and other dependencies retain separate licenses and costs.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows AGPL-3.0. AGPL-3.0 application code; Docker Desktop, selected models, Gemini and other dependencies retain separate licenses and costs.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

No-AI setup disables AI features. Source media mounts, background processing and cloud-provider choice matter for privacy and resource use. RAM/VRAM/disk minima and independently verified retrieval quality remain undocumented. [Source 1](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/IliasHad/edit-mind/blob/main/README.md)

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Studying or recreating a controllable neural style-transfer workflow, including blending styles and treating foreground/background separately.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/LICENSE)

The historical Titan X speed claim is specific to the author setup, not a current hardware guarantee. [Source](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Obtain the official source and a compatible Torch7 environment. CUDA/cuDNN, cunn and torch.cudnn are optional GPU dependencies; FFmpeg is needed for video. Follow the documented model-download script to obtain the decoder and modified VGG-19, checking their separate rights and availability. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A current reconstruction of the legacy environment and independent creator-use evidence remain incomplete. Research age alone does not justify an Experimental label, and the video path does not establish flicker-free output.

### How it uses AI

The original Torch7 implementation transfers an arbitrary reference style to a content image with adaptive instance normalization, with controls for style strength, color preservation and spatial masks. [Source](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

### Introduction

The original Torch7 implementation transfers an arbitrary reference style to a content image with adaptive instance normalization, with controls for style strength, color preservation and spatial masks. [Source 1](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

### What it is good for

Studying or recreating a controllable neural style-transfer workflow, including blending styles and treating foreground/background separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

### Demo & examples

The author README and ICCV paper include image comparisons and a video example. The historical Titan X speed claim is specific to the author setup, not a current hardware guarantee. [Source 1](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

### Install

Obtain the official source and a compatible Torch7 environment. CUDA/cuDNN, cunn and torch.cudnn are optional GPU dependencies; FFmpeg is needed for video. Follow the documented model-download script to obtain the decoder and modified VGG-19, checking their separate rights and availability. [Source 1](https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.06868)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Obtain the official source and a compatible Torch7 environment. CUDA/cuDNN, cunn and torch.cudnn are optional GPU dependencies; FFmpeg is needed for video. Follow the documented model-download script to obtain the decoder and modified VGG-19, checking their separate rights and availability.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/xunhuang1995/AdaIN-style.git
```


```sh
cd AdaIN-style
```


```sh
bash models/download_models.sh
```


```sh
th test.lua -content input/content/cornell.jpg -style input/style/woman_with_hat_matisse.jpg
```
