Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This is a specialized fork, not proof of a newly trained 3D foundation model. Checks cannot replace artistic/topology review, and the pinned Blender version constrains reuse.

### How it uses AI

A specialized AI coding harness drives a persistent headless Blender session with bpy scripts and staged validation, so an LLM can inspect and revise generated 3D assets. [Source](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

### Introduction

A specialized AI coding harness drives a persistent headless Blender session with bpy scripts and staged validation, so an LLM can inspect and revise generated 3D assets. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

### What it is good for

Supervised procedural asset generation in Blender with a record of scene changes and validation results. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

### Demo & examples

The README describes staged checks and the Blender session contract. A validation gate can catch some API/geometry errors but cannot establish aesthetic quality or a production-ready asset. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

### Install

The runtime harness pins Blender 5.2. Either review the official platform installer/release route or build from source using Bun. In a source checkout, install dependencies, set BLENDER_PATH to your Blender executable, bootstrap Blender Python dependencies, build libfive for your actual platform, and register the launcher. Run axiom3d from the output project folder. Windows instructions require a real Bash environment such as Git Bash, WSL or MSYS2. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

1. Install the pinned Blender 5.2 release and choose a documented binary or source route.
2. For a source build, use Bun and a compatible native build toolchain; replace the Blender path and platform placeholder with your own values.
3. Open the asset-output project directory and start axiom3d. Review generated geometry and exported files.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D.git
```


```sh
cd Axiom3D
```


```sh
bun install
```


```sh
export BLENDER_PATH="/path/to/blender"
```


```sh
blender --background --factory-startup --python blender/bootstrap.py
```


```sh
./blender/build-libfive.sh <linux-x64|linux-arm64|darwin-x64|darwin-arm64|windows-x64>
```


```sh
bun link
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

1. Install the documented pinned Blender/toolchain and start with a disposable asset project.
2. Ask for a simple mesh, inspect the staged checks and rendered views, then make a small revision.
3. Review topology, materials and scale in Blender and test export in the intended target engine.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/VossGloves/Axiom3D#requirements)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Blender 5.2 exactly in the current contract; Bun for development, Blender Python dependencies and libfive build requirements. A separate LLM model/provider is required.
- **Platforms:** Linux/macOS and Windows via WSL, Git Bash or MSYS2 setup routes are documented. They are not evidence that every Blender/backend combination was tested.

Media dependencies are not bundled: use the platform package-manager table, then run gallery check. Configure a small test archive in gallery.toml and opt into external AI classification only deliberately. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The documented source route builds the Tauri GUI and Rust CLI, then checks external media tools. Tagged releases are described as producing unsigned draft GUI/CLI bundles for Linux, Windows and both Mac architectures; this review does not establish that a particular draft asset is publicly downloadable. Media dependencies are not bundled: use the platform package-manager table, then run gallery check. Configure a small test archive in gallery.toml and opt into external AI classification only deliberately.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery
```


```sh
cd gallery
```


```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm tauri build --no-bundle
```


```sh
cargo build --release -p gallery-cli
```


```sh
gallery check
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with a backed-up, expendable sample folder and write a small ordered set of targets.
2. Preview classification and all destination operations; correct the AI descriptions and collision/metadata decisions.
3. Approve only the reviewed plan, verify resulting files and practice undo before exposing a larger archive.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Source build: recent stable Rust supporting edition 2024, Node.js and pnpm. Media tools: FFmpeg with libsvtav1/libopus, ffprobe, ExifTool, libavif, ImageMagick 7 and optipng for PNG presets; Linux GUI additionally uses GTK/WebKit/Tauri libraries. AI conditions use the external Anthropic service; a local-model route is not established by the reviewed Gallery guide.
- **Platforms:** Linux is the home platform. The README also documents Windows and macOS dependencies/bundles for Intel and Apple Silicon; app-menu/login integration is Linux-only. On macOS, restoring trashed originals is manual; Windows undo restores them automatically.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application. Encoder/tool licenses and the external Claude service are separate; local hosting does not make cloud classification offline.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application. Encoder/tool licenses and the external Claude service are separate; local hosting does not make cloud classification offline.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage and metered Anthropic calls only when an AI filter is used.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

AI conditions upload classification input to the selected provider. The application can eventually trash originals after its checks; keep independent backups and inspect the plan. Minimum system resources are not documented. The documented macOS undo does not automatically restore originals from Trash. Draft/unsigned bundles and author-written OS instructions are not independently tested releases. [Source 1](https://github.com/grigorii-horos/gallery/blob/main/README.md)

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototyping a procedural 3D object or spatial interaction once, then comparing its web, mobile and headset runtime routes.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official studios and current repository demonstrate scene editing and runtime adapters. The November 3 launch article is older than the current 1.4.0 source/release; its initial preview wording does not alone classify today’s code as Experimental. [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** For web integration install three-live/xr-live through npm, or obtain a pinned release bundle. The CLI can be installed from the three-live-compiler crate or an OS-specific release binary. Native Android and Apple integrations have separate JitPack/AAR and SwiftPM/XCFramework instructions; choose one route rather than installing every toolchain. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 software. Three.js/engine dependencies, mobile OS SDKs, hosted endpoints and chosen model/texture permissions remain separate. No foundation-model weights are included. Local compile/render compute; any selected language/image/voice provider incurs its own model or service costs. [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This is not an officially supported Google product. Compiled-scene quotas, lints and author sandbox claims are not an independent security or quality audit. Static Scene Viewer/Quick Look handoff does not preserve every live interaction; browser WebXR and native AR are different routes. The changed default-branch documentation expands optional scientific geometry kits and texture controls. With an image endpoint configured, missing textures may generate automatically; ai-textures="manual" selects explicit generation. Pin a matching release/bundle and assess costs and interactions on that route.

### How it uses AI

An AI scene-authoring toolkit compiles an external model’s procedural Three.js into portable 3D assets and interactive native/web scenes, with CLI/MCP inspection and image-to-scene/editing integrations. [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

### Introduction

An AI scene-authoring toolkit compiles an external model’s procedural Three.js into portable 3D assets and interactive native/web scenes, with CLI/MCP inspection and image-to-scene/editing integrations. [Source 1](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source 3](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source 4](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source 5](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

### What it is good for

Prototyping a procedural 3D object or spatial interaction once, then comparing its web, mobile and headset runtime routes. [Source 1](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source 3](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source 4](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source 5](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

### Demo & examples

The official studios and current repository demonstrate scene editing and runtime adapters. The November 3 launch article is older than the current 1.4.0 source/release; its initial preview wording does not alone classify today’s code as Experimental. [Source 1](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source 3](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source 4](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source 5](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

### Install

For web integration install three-live/xr-live through npm, or obtain a pinned release bundle. The CLI can be installed from the three-live-compiler crate or an OS-specific release binary. Native Android and Apple integrations have separate JitPack/AAR and SwiftPM/XCFramework instructions; choose one route rather than installing every toolchain. [Source 1](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source 3](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source 4](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source 5](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For web integration install three-live/xr-live through npm, or obtain a pinned release bundle. The CLI can be installed from the three-live-compiler crate or an OS-specific release binary. Native Android and Apple integrations have separate JitPack/AAR and SwiftPM/XCFramework instructions; choose one route rather than installing every toolchain.

```sh
npm install three-live xr-live
```


```sh
cargo install three-live-compiler --bin three-live-cli
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/google/three-live/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://google.github.io/three-live/three/) [Source 3](https://google.github.io/three-live/xr/) [Source 4](https://github.com/google/three-live/releases/tag/v1.4.0) [Source 5](https://opensource.googleblog.com/2026/11/introducing-three-live-ai-generated-3d-ar-webxr.html)

1. Start from an official small scene and your chosen runtime route.
2. Use the documented agent/MCP or configured AI endpoint for a narrow scene edit, and inspect the contact sheet and spatial diagnostics.
3. Export GLB/USDZ or test the live scene on the target device; verify interactions separately from a static AR handoff.

## Koaia

Performance, projection & stage media · Interactive, immersive & live media · Images & design · Physical, robotic & kinetic installations

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Auditioning model-driven visual treatments for a performance or installation, while keeping the input layer and result visible.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-11. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete root text: a GPLv3 body with a changed explanatory URL, attribution/runtime notices and an appended CeCILL provenance notice. The latter explicitly says the whole distribution is treated as GPLv3; no noncommercial or purpose restriction is added. Dependencies and models retain separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/koaia/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official documentation shows input and AI output. Its quick start and the repository supply specific CUDA/VRAM requirements; no independent latency/visual-quality review was established. [Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/koaia/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Follow SAT’s official download/setup route for Windows/Linux, or its source build instructions. Current Suite SAT ready-to-install Creator builds have subscription/account conditions, distinct from the public source grant. In the GUI, build a TensorRT engine from a permitted model in MODEL, then load it in RUN. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/koaia/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete root text contains GPLv3 plus attribution/third-party notices and a CeCILL provenance notice that explicitly treats the whole distribution as GPLv3. These are software terms; model, CUDA/TensorRT and subscription-build conditions remain separate. Local GPU compute/model storage. Current ready-to-install Creator builds use SAT account/subscription terms; the public GPL source remains separately available. [Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/koaia/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The public README still marks NDI/Syphon/Spout streaming as coming soon; do not plan a show around that unverified output route. A subscription download route is not the same as unrestricted model permissions or a tested source build.

[Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/livepose/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official quick start documents model loading, camera selection and OSC output. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete root license combines GPLv3 software terms with retained third-party attribution and a CeCILL provenance notice explicitly treating the whole distribution as GPLv3. ONNX is a format, not a license for every model. Local inference/camera compute; SAT ready-to-install Creator builds have current account/subscription conditions. Optional proprietary creative hosts and models have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/sat-mtl/livepose/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/installation.html) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Pose jitter, occlusion, re-identification and latency need testing in the intended space. - **Cost:** Local inference/camera compute; SAT ready-to-install Creator builds have current account/subscription conditions. Optional proprietary creative hosts and models have separate terms.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/sat-mtl/livepose/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source 3](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/installation.html) [Source 4](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)

### Limitations

Pose jitter, occlusion, re-identification and latency need testing in the intended space. Max/MSP and TouchDesigner are optional separately licensed hosts; open OSC recipients are also supported. Independent production use and complete hardware requirements remain gaps. [Source 1](https://github.com/sat-mtl/livepose/blob/master/README.md) [Source 2](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source 3](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/installation.html) [Source 4](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/)

Preserve notices. Model checkpoints and upstream dependencies retain separate terms and require their own review. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** Author video edits and parity/performance measurements are described, not independently assessed. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone, uv, checkpoint and conversion instructions are present; independent reproduction and quantized-memory verification remain gaps. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers were not independently established.
- **Independent use · unknown:** No independent first-hand production use was established for this exact MLX editing route.

Documentation review only; no discovered software was installed or run. Quantized memory claims need route-specific verification; full model terms and independent first-hand use remain unresolved.

### How it uses AI

A 16B video diffusion model and vision-language conditioning edit a source clip from a natural-language prompt, including local changes and scene restyling. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

### Introduction

An Apple MLX port of JoyAI-Video-Edit runs instruction-guided source-video edits with a diffusion transformer, causal video VAE and MiMo-VL conditioning. It is an offline/near-line editor on the documented Mac hardware. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

### What it is good for

Testing prompt-guided restyles, local changes, background edits and reference-image-conditioned clip edits on an adequately provisioned Apple Silicon machine. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

### Demo & examples

The author reports numerical/parity checks and real 480p edits on an M3 Ultra. Its measured roughly 0.9 FPS is an author result, not upstream real-time GPU speed or our own benchmark. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

### Install

Clone the official GitLab source, create the uv environment, download JoyAI and MiMo checkpoints from their upstreams and convert them once using the documented joyai_edit.convert command. The model files are not bundled. Read the raw/checkpoint and converted-path instructions before the large download. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

1. Install macOS 14+, Python 3.12+ and uv on Apple Silicon.
2. Clone the official repository and run uv sync.
3. Follow the README checkpoint-download and conversion steps, keeping source and converted folders explicit.

```sh
git clone https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx.git
```


```sh
cd joyai-video-edit-mlx
```


```sh
uv sync
```

### First project

Use a short source clip and the converted weights to test a narrow edit before a long batch. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

1. Run the documented editor CLI with input video, a short instruction, output path and --weights folder.
2. Alternatively start uv run joyai-edit-server --port 8765 and use its local video/webcam UI.
3. Compare requested edits, identity/temporal consistency and sound handling with the source, then review the exported frames.
### Hardware & software

Keep author measurements, planned/quantized routes and tested baseline distinct. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

- **Hardware:** The README reports roughly 57 GB for the full bf16 working set (64 GB comfortable, 96 GB+ preferred) and about 120 GB disk for raw plus converted weights. Its q4/32 GB descriptions are inconsistent between the requirements list and later performance section; treat that route as needing verification, not a proven minimum.
- **Software:** macOS 14+, Python 3.12+, uv and MLX 0.31+ in the documented environment. Source model downloads and conversion are required. Development/parity dependencies are separate.
- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon macOS. This MLX port does not establish Windows, Intel Mac or Linux support.

### License, model weights & costs

Full software grant reviewed; model rights remain separate. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** JoyAI-Video-Edit and MiMo-VL checkpoints are supplied by their respective upstreams. Their complete weight terms were not established in this review.
- **Commercial:** Apache-2.0 applies to this port’s code. Do not infer commercial model/output permissions from that code grant.
- **Cost:** Local Mac compute and substantial model storage. No paid inference API is required for the documented local route.

### Why it merits attention

Included for a concrete, documented local AI editing workflow. Author parity checks are not independent creator-use or output-quality evidence. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

### Limitations

This is not the upstream 24 FPS datacenter-GPU workflow. Performance, memory, prompt adherence and quantized behavior must be checked against the exact local hardware and material; model permissions and independent reproduction remain gaps. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE)

Video, animation & film · Audio, music & voice · Editing, captions & post-production · Accessible media & assistive creation

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Scope: Making a simple captioned MP4 from an owned video and a separate voice track, with the caption style editable in source.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/LICENSE)

The inspected app.py establishes the actual Whisper/MoviePy path, not independently verified transcription or a benchmark-quality demo. Whisper model/runtime permissions and MoviePy/FFmpeg/font/input-media terms remain separate; the repository does not bundle every dependency. [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. One video and one extra track per execution; filenames, model and caption styling are fixed in the script. No GUI, independent reproduction or output-quality evaluation is established. Missing inputs, silent source video and unpinned dependency compatibility are not resolved by the README.

### How it uses AI

A local Python script uses the Whisper base neural speech model to derive segment timestamps, then burns captions onto real footage and mixes an additional voice track with MoviePy. [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Introduction

A local Python script uses the Whisper base neural speech model to derive segment timestamps, then burns captions onto real footage and mixes an additional voice track with MoviePy. [Source 1](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### What it is good for

Making a simple captioned MP4 from an owned video and a separate voice track, with the caption style editable in source. [Source 1](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Demo & examples

The README includes a workflow illustration and specifies output.mp4. The inspected app.py establishes the actual Whisper/MoviePy path, not independently verified transcription or a benchmark-quality demo. [Source 1](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Install

Clone the source, create and activate a Python virtual environment, install the two unpinned requirements, and install FFmpeg separately on PATH. Place video.mp4 and voice.mp3 in the project directory. Windows activation uses .venv\Scripts\activate; macOS/Linux use source .venv/bin/activate. The first Whisper run may download its model. [Source 1](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source, create and activate a Python virtual environment, install the two unpinned requirements, and install FFmpeg separately on PATH. Place video.mp4 and voice.mp3 in the project directory. Windows activation uses .venv\Scripts\activate; macOS/Linux use source .venv/bin/activate. The first Whisper run may download its model.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge.git
```


```sh
cd ClipForge
```


```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python app.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/app.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/abenezerwassefa-byte/ClipForge/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Activate the new environment before installing requirements; choose a small owned clip with a spoken audio track.
2. Place video.mp4 and voice.mp3 beside app.py. The current source hardcodes these names, uses Whisper base, sets fp16=False and scales the extra voice track to 0.2.
3. Run python app.py, inspect the burned captions, segment timing and audio balance in output.mp4, and edit the source style/settings if needed. Keep originals.

No embedded NLP model, universal browser support or independently validated readability is claimed.

### SpriteForge · MIT

Preparing a draft animated sprite sheet or multiple game-asset views for manual cleanup.
A sprite workspace combines Gemini image generation, Veo animation, video-to-frame extraction and neural rembg background removal with crop/resize and asset-library tools.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. MIT application code; Gemini/Veo access, rembg/u2net weights and source assets have separate terms and costs. Frame extraction and a ZIP export do not establish clean sprite motion, alpha edges or engine readiness.
